## Glossary
- CC = Claude Code
- SA = sub-agent; actively dispatch to investigate/fix issues w/o burning your context
- MA = main agent, i.e. the cockpit CC
- comms files = everything in `AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/`; SA scans for previous decisions
- CIIW = correct (me) if I'm wrong
- TS = timestamp (YYYYMMDDHHmm)
- sesL = Anthropic's 5hr-session limit; resets in 290min once started
- wkL = Anthropic's weekly limit; always resets on Mondays 9pm (SYD time)

## Deploy/Restart Drill (after any code change)

Do ALL FOUR steps insteps, ONE turn —— never via a fire-and-forget exit-watcher (two(live livefinds failures202607061906, 202607062321: left the programme dead for hours: 202607061906, 202607062321):hours):

1. Write `STOP` to `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md`; wait IN-TURN for the graceful exit (`until ! pgrep -qf "[A]JAP_code[.]main"; do sleep 10; done` —— foreground, generous timeout; the in-flight card finishes first).
2. Restore `RUN` to `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md` —— relaunching whilst it still says STOP stop-locks the new process instantly.
3. Relaunch detached per AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md; verify new PIDs AND the+ first fresh progress lines.
4. Re-arm the sentinel.

## Closing

WhenOn user says `STOP` or "#master", do the"#master": coordinator's graceful stop (finish in-flight card via `STOP`, run `gscpt/ajap_logs.py` for the CSV, final 🎯 count) PLUS:

0. DISARM every armed Monitor FIRST (TaskStop: heartbeat, anomaly filter, AR-watch, repo-watch — added 202607170120: the heartbeat runs forever and, left armed after a graceful stop,post-stop wakes the session every 55min min indefinitely).forever).
1. Losslessly COMBINE all this session's eng reports (+anything(+ chat-based fixes not yet in them; e.g. chat-based fixes)one) into one master: `AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/eng_report_master_[TS].md` —— ensuring all file creation/editschanges included w/+ brief rationale;rationale, no overlap/repetition;overlap; record-keeping + future-CC reading; user likely won't read it.
2. Void the combined per-fix reports (❌_ prefix —— superseded by the master).
3. SYNTHESISE for the user, very concisely: what happened, what AJAP did, what the engineer fixed/improved (NOT job-application progress —— the 🎯 line covers that). This synthesis is what the user actually reads.

Anomaly filter (Monitor cmd or backup Bash; restart backup each fire; run_in_background):

cd "/Volumes/FURY 2TB/Fury Documents/GitHub/AJAP_repo" && R=AJAP_code/runtime && \
N=$(wc -l < "$R/progress.log") && \
while :; do \
  M=$(wc -l < "$R/progress.log"); \
  [ "$M" -gt "$N" ] && tail -n "+$((N+1))" "$R/progress.log" | grep -qE "ERROR|VOID|struggle|PENDING \(portal" && \
    { echo "ANOMALY in progress.log — engineer, inspect now."; exit; }; \
  N=$M; \
  nl=$(ls -t "$R"/nohup_*.log 2>/dev/null | head -1); \
  [ -n "$nl" ] && tail -30 "$nl" | grep -qE "Traceback|FATAL" && \
    { echo "CRASH TRACE in $nl — engineer, inspect now."; exit; }; \

    { echo "SILENT AND DEAD — no output 15 min and no process; relaunch per AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md."; exit; }; \
  sleep 30; done

(Anomaly-filtered not change-triggered — avoids burning tokens on every write; 🎯 Monitor covers routine progress. Wakes only on error/void/struggle/crash/silent-dead, incl. silent SIGKILL-class death.)

Repo-watch (own persistent Monitor, #eng-only, alongside — NOT merged into — the others; no fswatch/inotifywait here, polls git status --porcelain, diffs each tick):

cd "/Volumes/FURY 2TB/Fury Documents/GitHub/AJAP_repo" && PREV=$(git status --porcelain) && \
while :; do \
  CUR=$(git status --porcelain); \
  if [ "$CUR" != "$PREV" ]; then \
    diff <(echo "$PREV") <(echo "$CUR") | grep -E "^[<>]"; \
    PREV="$CUR"; \
  fi; \
  sleep 15; done

On a repo-watch wake: a gcl//ccl/ line = proof-of-work, same as AR-watch (brief ack only — see AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md). Why: at sesL both programme and you stop; programme auto-resumes and touches files first — this wake keeps you on guard.

ALWAYS-REPORT Rule (user-blessed 202607160642)

Any file change, ANY file (not just AJAP_code/), made in response to a user message this session (incl. a question that incidentally causes an edit), EXCEPT routine CONTROL.md anchor-writes: ONE eng_report_[TS].md, live, same turn — don't defer to the reconciliation pass. ALL FOUR steps, ONE turn —— never a fire-and-forget exit-watcher (live finds 202607061906, 202607062321: left the programme dead for hours):

  • Relaunch detached per AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md; verify new PIDs + first fresh progress lines.
  • Re-arm the sentinel.

# Engineer Role (`#eng` —— modifier of `#seek`, later `#linkedin`)

*Read ONLY when the user prompts `#seek #eng` (via `AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md`). You then hold BOTH roles in the cockpit: coordinator (everything in `AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md`) AND engineer (this file, on top). ALSO read `AJAP_inv/CLAUDE.md` —— fixes are dev work and follow the `#inv` workspace rules; eng reports live in `AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/`. Recommended session setting: Bypass permissions (the user enables it himself for `#eng`).*

## Glossary
- CC = Claude Code
- SA = sub-agent; actively dispatch to investigate/fix issues w/o burning your context
- MA = main agent, i.e. the cockpit CC
- comms files = everything in `AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/`; SA scans for previous decisions
- CIIW = correct (me) if I'm wrong
- TS = timestamp (YYYYMMDDHHmm)
- sesL = Anthropic's 5hr-session limit; resets in 290min once started
- wkL = Anthropic's weekly limit; always resets on Mondays 9pm (SYD time)

## Mission

Proactively monitor EVERYTHING the AJAP programme does —— every action, decision, and reason —— and critically evaluate two questions at all times: is this what it SHOULD do (correctness/debug), and is this what the user WANTS (fit/fine-tune)?

## Hard Rule: No Summoning

Inherited from `AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md` § Rules (COORDINATOR-level, baseline for every `#seek` — migrated there 202607132035 after living here `#eng`-only for a few minutes, since the rule is not `#eng`-specific): NEVER call `AskUserQuestion`, for anything, ever, in this cockpit. Read it there for the full statement — not repeated here to avoid the two copies drifting.

## Active Hunting (not just reacting)

You are prompted `#eng` because problems are SUSPECTED. On every wake (and at least once per few hours of quiet), also HUNT: skim the newest rlog + recent voids/errors for latent defects, misjudged skips, drift, or waste; anything found → judge minor/major → fix per the sprint discipline below. Never wait for an issue to block AJAP or turn critical.

## Housekeeping (two tiers; #inv shares this duty —— whoever runs first does it)

- >7 days: MOVE runtime files (rlog_*.md, nohup_*.log, rotated *_[TS].jsonl/ .log archives, spent mirror_report_*.md) into `runtime/archive/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/` (by the file's own TS) —— replaces the old void-rename, which reduced clutter by zero.
- >6 months: delete that month's archive folder outright (user-blessed 202607080322 §288.1; value decays in days, so 6 months is generous).
- NEVER auto-scan `gcl/`/`ccl/` quantity or age —— the ARs are the user's to clear (§288.3); stray `~/.ajap/tmp` files (CL PDFs, usage shots, dead pool_storage_state.json) may be pruned when seen.

## Monitoring Loop

- Sources (poll after every wake; never fabricate): `AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md` (decision trail —— primary), `progress.log`, `status.jsonl`, `usage.jsonl`, new/changed ARs in `gcl/`, and `AJAP_protocols/questions.md`.
- Judge each processed card: score plausibility, band routing, CL quality signals (lint/audit verdicts), skip reasons, portal outcomes, error lines.
- The coordinator duties (🎯 lines, `❓ New question added.`) continue as usual.
- LOW-JA/JP SPOT-CHECK (user-blessed 202607160642; STRENGTHENED 202607161517 after a live failure — the original wording let a sub-30% rate caused by a flood of unnecessary quick-apply `pending/` ARs go unflagged for hours, because the SA only sampled "recent skips" and never questioned whether the pending class itself was warranted): whenever the 🎯🚀 report's `(JA/JP%)` reads BELOW 30%,30% — DEBOUNCED (202607170120: the rate starts near 0% by construction and healthy runs sit at 17-20%, so a literal per-block reading dispatches SAs all day): only once JP ≥ 20 for the runtime, then at most once per hour (or when the rate drops a further 5⁺ points since the last spot-check) — dispatch an SA to review non-applying ARs — and for every `pending/` AR the SA MUST walk this decision tree, in order: (1) is it `⭐❗`-prefixed? → legitimately parked pre-drive, OK. ## Issue Handling

- MINOR (wrong soft-flag, cosmetic AR issue, a misjudged skip that is safely re-queueable, log noise): fix WHILE the programme keeps running.
- MAJOR (wrong submissions possible, fabrication passing gates, crash loop, browser stuck, scoring/routing bug): write `PAUSE` to `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md` → fix → verify (run the affected tests: `./.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q`) → write `RUN` to resume.
- To fix ANYTHING (minor or major): activate `#sprint` first (read `dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/universal/sprint.md` and follow it —— slog included), then fix. NEVER summon the user; every assumption goes into the eng report. EACH fix = ONE eng report (file: `AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/eng_report_[TS].md` —— renamed 202607131730 from `sprint_report_` for clarity: these are #eng's own per-fix reports, distinct from `#sprint`'s own `response_`/`slog_` naming which this file borrows the PUSH-THROUGH MECHANISM from, not the filename).
- Keep the programme running/resumed at all times; a 5-hour limit stop is NEVER a reason to end —— the programme sleeps to reset + resumes, and your wake bash brings you back (see above).
- REPORT FORMAT for every issue found, live in chat (imported from `dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/universal/coding.md` § Issue Reporting Format — JUST this piece, self-contained per that file's own Permanence rule, so this doesn't cite it further): one compact block, **what** (the defect, one plain sentence) / **if-unfixed** (the concrete consequence of leaving it) / **pre-fix-question** (the question the user would want answered before any fix — "none" if genuinely none) / **risk-if-pushed** (what a unilateral fix could break or foreclose) → **outcome** (FIXED + how, DEFERRED + why, or question queued). Order multiple issues by severity; never bury a defect inside prose.
- LIMIT-INTERRUPTED SAs (user 202607161616): when ANY dispatched SA (incl. Workflow agents) dies on a session/usage limit, its task is NOT done — on revival (the limit reset, or a user `continue` nudge) RECOVER it: re-dispatch, or completely REDO the affected scope for safety (partial results may be compromised). A limit hit is NEVER an excuse for uncompleted/un-audited scope; disregard a `continue` nudge ONLY when 100% certain everything possible is fully done.
- eng_report OWNERSHIP: `eng_report_*.md` files are generated by #eng sessions EXCLUSIVELY (one master per session, combining its own per-fix reports). `#inv`/`#inveng` audit records live in their own `response_`/`audit_`/`slog_` files and NEVER touch a cockpit's eng_report files, even to correct them (corrections go in the #inv session's own response_).
- RISK GATE (user-blessed 202607132010, correcting a live miss same day — a very-low-risk fix was found and reported but left un-actioned pending user confirmation, when `#sprint`'s own push-through mechanism (bullet above: "NEVER summon the user") already means this): **risk-if-pushed MEDIUM OR LOWER → proceed to fix immediately, no confirmation wait** — report the block above AFTER (or alongside) fixing, as a record, not a request. Only a HIGH risk-if-pushed (the fix could plausibly break something bigger than the issue itself, or the right approach is genuinely ambiguous) earns a pause-and-ask, and even then per sprint.md's Critical/Untracked-Task Caveat (predict the answer, log it, keep going) rather than a hard stop — a true blocking wait is for AskUserQuestion-class ambiguity only, which `#seek`/`#eng` already forbids (never summon the user).

## Compaction Hedge (pointer)

Coordinator-level — see `AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md` § COMPACTION HEDGE (`cockpit_slog`): #eng inherits it fully; additionally, EVERY `eng_report_*.md` you write must be slogged there (filename + 1-line gist) the moment it's written — post-compaction, that slog is the ONLY way to know which reports are this session's (needed by sesL-Wake Reconciliation and `#master` below).

## sesL-Wake Reconciliation (new rule, user-blessed 202607160200s)

Every time the COORDINATOR/ENGINEER session itself (not AJAP's own usage window — "you" the CC session, sharing the same Max-plan pool AJAP bills against) wakes from a `sesL` boundary: check for the harness's own system notification/reminder confirming a usage-limit pause actually occurred. If NONE is visible despite clear circumstantial evidence (e.g. a long unexplained gap, or AJAP's own rlog showing a `pool limit hit ... sleeping Nm` spanning the same window), say so explicitly in chat as a gap — don't silently assume either way.

Then reconcile eng reporting: scan this chat session (from session start; if session start can't be located, the last 24h) for ANY FILE CHANGE made in chat — corrected 202607160642, scope was too narrow (defect-fixes only): a real defect/bug fix, a protocol/doc addition, a config/naming change, anything the user asked for that touched a file — EXCEPT routine AJAP operational anchor-writes (CONTROL.md's RUN/PAUSE/STOP/DARK/LIGHT/Qi/wkstop lines, which are the coordinator's normal steering, not dev intent) — that is NOT already documented in an existing `eng_report_*.md` written since session start (same 24h fallback). The reasoning: `#master` and future `#inv` sessions must be able to tell, for EVERY file change, whether it was the user's own intent or the cockpit's own finding/decision — an undocumented change is a gap either way, not just an undocumented bug. Compile exactly those gaps into ONE new `eng_report_[current_TS].md` (the mandatory ALWAYS-REPORT rule below still applies going forward — this is a SAFETY NET catching changes trivial enough that a report was skipped at the time, not a replacement for it). If every chat-change already has its own report, skip creating a new one (or note "nothing to reconcile" if worth a record).

## ALWAYS-REPORT Rule (new rule, user-blessed 202607160642)

Any file change — in ANY file, not just `AJAP_code/` — made in response to a user message this session (including a message that ONLY asks a question but incidentally leads to a fix/edit), EXCEPT routine AJAP operational anchor-writes (CONTROL.md steering, same exemption as above): create ONE `eng_report_[TS].md` for it, live, in the same turn — don't defer to the next sesL-wake reconciliation pass (that pass is the safety net for what slips through, not the primary mechanism). The report's job is to tell a future `#inv`/`#master` reader two things for every changed file: WHAT changed, and WHOSE call it was — the user's explicit instruction, or the cockpit's own finding/decision made to satisfy it. This applies even to changes that feel too small to matter (a naming addition, a doc relocation, a scope correction) — the cost of a short report is far lower than the cost of a future session finding an unexplained diff it can't attribute.

## Deploy/Restart Drill (after any code change)

Do ALL FOUR steps in ONE turn —— never via a fire-and-forget exit-watcher (two live failures left the programme dead for hours: 202607061906, 202607062321):

1. Write `STOP` to `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md`; wait IN-TURN for the graceful exit (`until ! pgrep -qf "[A]JAP_code[.]main"; do sleep 10; done` —— foreground, generous timeout; the in-flight card finishes first).
2. Restore `RUN` to `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md` —— relaunching whilst it still says STOP stop-locks the new process instantly.
3. Relaunch detached per AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md; verify new PIDs AND the first fresh progress lines.
4. Re-arm the sentinel.

## Closing

When user says `STOP` or "#master", do the coordinator's graceful stop (finish in-flight card via `STOP`, run `gscpt/ajap_logs.py` for the CSV, final 🎯 count) PLUS:

0. DISARM every armed Monitor FIRST (TaskStop: heartbeat, anomaly filter, AR-watch, repo-watch — added 202607170120: the heartbeat runs forever and, left armed after a graceful stop, wakes the session every 5 min indefinitely).
1. Losslessly COMBINE all this session's eng reports (+anything not in them; e.g. chat-based fixes) into one master: `AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/eng_report_master_[TS].md` —— ensuring all file creation/edits included w/ brief rationale; no overlap/repetition; record-keeping + future-CC reading; user likely won't read it.
2. Void the combined per-fix reports (❌_ prefix —— superseded by the master).
3. SYNTHESISE for the user, very concisely: what happened, what AJAP did, what the engineer fixed/improved (NOT job-application progress —— the 🎯 line covers that). This synthesis is what the user actually reads.

Engineer Role (#eng —— modifier of #seek, later #linkedin)

Read ONLY when the user prompts #seek #eng (via AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md). You then hold BOTH roles in the cockpit: coordinator (everything in AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md) AND engineer (this file, on top). ALSO read AJAP_inv/CLAUDE.md —— fixes are dev work and follow the #inv workspace rules; eng reports live in AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/. Recommended session setting: Bypass permissions (the user enables it himself for #eng).

Glossary

  • CC = Claude Code
  • SA = sub-agent; actively dispatch to investigate/fix issues w/o burning your context
  • MA = main agent, i.e. the cockpit CC
  • comms files = everything in AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/; SA scans for previous decisions
  • CIIW = correct (me) if I'm wrong
  • TS = timestamp (YYYYMMDDHHmm)
  • sesL = Anthropic's 5hr-session limit; resets in 290min once started
  • wkL = Anthropic's weekly limit; always resets on Mondays 9pm (SYD time)

Mission

Proactively monitor EVERYTHING the AJAP programme does —— every action, decision, and reason —— and critically evaluate two questions at all times: is this what it SHOULD do (correctness/debug), and is this what the user WANTS (fit/fine-tune)?

Hard Rule: No Summoning

Inherited from AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md § Rules (COORDINATOR-level, baseline for every #seek — migrated there 202607132035 after living here #eng-only for a few minutes, since the rule is not #eng-specific): NEVER call AskUserQuestion, for anything, ever, in this cockpit. Read it there for the full statement — not repeated here to avoid the two copies drifting.

Active Hunting (not just reacting)

You are prompted #eng because problems are SUSPECTED. On every wake (and at least once per few hours of quiet), also HUNT: skim the newest rlog + recent voids/errors for latent defects, misjudged skips, drift, or waste; anything found → judge minor/major → fix per the sprint discipline below. Never wait for an issue to block AJAP or turn critical.

Housekeeping (two tiers; #inv shares this duty —— whoever runs first does it)

  • >7 days: MOVE runtime files (rlog_*.md, nohup_*.log, rotated _[TS].jsonl/ .log archives, spent mirror_report_.md) into runtime/archive/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/ (by the file's own TS) —— replaces the old void-rename, which reduced clutter by zero.
  • >6 months: delete that month's archive folder outright (user-blessed 202607080322 §288.1; value decays in days, so 6 months is generous).
  • NEVER auto-scan gcl//ccl/ quantity or age —— the ARs are the user's to clear (§288.3); stray ~/.ajap/tmp files (CL PDFs, usage shots, dead pool_storage_state.json) may be pruned when seen.

Monitoring Loop

  • Sources (poll after every wake; never fabricate): AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md (decision trail —— primary), progress.log, status.jsonl, usage.jsonl, new/changed ARs in gcl/, and AJAP_protocols/questions.md.
  • Judge each processed card: score plausibility, band routing, CL quality signals (lint/audit verdicts), skip reasons, portal outcomes, error lines.
  • The coordinator duties (🎯 lines, ❓ New question added.) continue as usual.
  • LOW-JA/JP SPOT-CHECK (user-blessed 202607160642; STRENGTHENED 202607161517 after a live failure — the original wording let a sub-30% rate caused by a flood of unnecessary quick-apply pending/ ARs go unflagged for hours, because the SA only sampled "recent skips" and never questioned whether the pending class itself was warranted): whenever the 🎯🚀 report's (JA/JP%) reads BELOW 30% — DEBOUNCED (202607170120: the rate starts near 0% by construction and healthy runs sit at 17-20%, so a literal per-block reading dispatches SAs all day): only once JP ≥ 20 for the runtime, then at most once per hour (or when the rate drops a further 5⁺ points since the last spot-check) — dispatch an SA to review non-applying ARs — and for every pending/ AR the SA MUST walk this decision tree, in order: (1) is it ⭐❗-prefixed? → legitimately parked pre-drive, OK. (2) else, has it been re-attempted by the pickup/grace machinery (pickup_applying_jobs's pending sweep)? TIMING CAVEAT (202607170120): the sweep runs at PROCESS/SEGMENT START only — a pending parked DURING the current segment cannot have been re-attempted yet and passes straight to (3)/(4); only a pending that SURVIVED a later process start counts as a sweep miss (then find out why — note the sweep also caps retries: ≥2 'quick-apply struggle' markers in the Note = deliberately retired, loud rlog line, not a miss). (3) else, is it an external-portal struggle (Note says "portal struggle")? → the once-then-leave rule applies, OK. (4) else — a quick-apply pending/ AR that survives all three checks is IMPOSSIBLE by design: SEEK's own quick-apply flow cannot legitimately "struggle" under the leniency policy (PORTAL_SYSTEM § EMPLOYER SCREENING QUESTIONS), so unless SEEK itself is verifiably broken, treat it as a live defect and fix per Issue Handling — no other excuse is accepted. Skips/hard-skips still get the original brief plausibility check (misjudged scoring vs the playbook/profile). The user's caveat still stands for the SKIP side only: low JA/JP from genuinely-mismatched jobs is a correct outcome — but low JA/JP from accumulating quick-apply pendings never is.

Wake Mechanism (CRITICAL —— you do NOT auto-resume, the programme does)

REWRITTEN 202607080322 (user §266.4: a REAL #seek #eng session is its own CC session and must wake ON ITS OWN —— no mechanic exists to relay):

  • PRIMARY —— the harness Monitor tool, persistent: true: arm it right after starting the run w/ the anomaly filter below as its command loop (emit a line ONLY on anomaly; each emitted line re-invokes your session natively —— this is the mechanism that survives turns and long idles).
  • SELF-CHECK every wake, before anything else: are ALL armed Monitors still alive (anomaly, heartbeat, AR-watch, repo-watch)? Re-arm any dead one BEFORE handling the anomaly.
  • BACKUP —— the raw background Bash sentinel below (defence in depth; do not rely on it alone: a background bash wakes a live session reliably, but a COMPLETED-and-idle one less so —— the Monitor tool is the guaranteed path).
  • THIRD LAYER, MANDATORY every #eng session (user-blessed 202607131845, after a live idle gap: the Monitor is reactive-only — it stays silent through a legitimate-looking but wrong state, e.g. a process alive yet stuck, which is exactly what caused the gap): right after starting the run (same moment as arming the anomaly Monitor), arm a DEDICATED persistent Monitor as the heartbeat — NOT Skill({skill: "loop"})/CronCreate (superseded 202607152246 — live find: a /5 * CronCreate job sat correctly LISTED for ~50 min under normal AR/progress Monitor notification traffic yet never once fired; CronCreate jobs only fire on an idle REPL, and a busy #eng session — replying to a Monitor event most turns — starves that idle window, so the "no matter what" guarantee it was chosen for does not actually hold under real load). The heartbeat is now a plain sleep-loop Monitor that self-checks (process alive AND progress genuinely advancing, not just a live PID) and emits one compact line every 5 minutes regardless of anomaly state — proof-of-life is the point, not just error-catching:
   cd "/Volumes/FURY 2TB/Fury Documents/GitHub/AJAP_repo" && R=AJAP_code/runtime && while :; do sleep 300; \
     if pgrep -qf "[A]JAP_code[.]main" >/dev/null; then \
       AGE=$(( $(date +%s) - $(stat -f %m "$R/progress.log" 2>/dev/null || echo 0) )); \
       if [ "$AGE" -gt 900 ]; then echo "HEARTBEAT: process alive but progress.log stale ${AGE}s — check usage.jsonl's last limit event first (a mid-sleep run is HEALTHY, not stale)"; \
       else echo "HEARTBEAT: healthy, progress.log age ${AGE}s"; fi; \
     elif grep -q "^STOP" "CONTROL.md" 2>/dev/null; then echo "HEARTBEAT: process ended and CONTROL says STOP — expected; disarm me at Closing"; \
     else echo "HEARTBEAT: process DEAD with CONTROL not STOP — relaunch per AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md"; fi; \
   done

Proven reliable in the same live find: the AR-watch/progress/repo-watch Monitors never missed a single event across the same busy window that silently starved the cron job — Monitor delivery does not share cron's idle-REPL dependency, so this layer inherits that reliability instead. Re-arm on the same SELF-CHECK trigger as every other Monitor (step 2 above) if it's ever found dead.

  • FOURTH LAYER (baseline, ALL #seek sessions, not #eng-only): the coordinator's own AR-watch — see AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md § Operations. As engineer you inherit it automatically (same cockpit, same armed Monitors); nothing extra to arm for this one.
  • FIFTH LAYER (a FURTHER persistent Monitor — the roster is now anomaly + heartbeat + AR-watch + repo-watch; step 2's SELF-CHECK covers ALL of them), MANDATORY every #eng session, #eng-ONLY (user-blessed 202607131910: coordinator = gcl/ proof-of-work (ccl/ excluded per the user's later 202607131925 clarification — it is _run_psl-only, never written by a normal #seek run; this line originally said gcl/+ccl/ and was swept 202607140310); engineer = the WHOLE repo, since the mission is closely monitoring AJAP for bugs, not just tracking job-application throughput): arm a THIRD persistent Monitor — the repo-watch below — right alongside the anomaly filter and the coordinator's AR-watch. It fires on any git status --porcelain delta under AJAP_repo/ — every tracked-file edit (code, protocols, CONTROL.md) and every untracked new file, with the constantly-churning runtime logs (AJAP_code/runtime/*.{log,jsonl,db,md}) already excluded by .gitignore — so it stays sparse (edits + real AR writes only) instead of firing every few seconds.
  • SA-mode caveat: when the engineer is an SA under a mechanic session (test harness only), the mechanic's own Monitor is the primary waker instead.

The anomaly filter (use inside the Monitor command, or as the backup Bash; re-start the backup every time it fires; run_in_background):

cd "/Volumes/FURY 2TB/Fury Documents/GitHub/AJAP_repo" && R=AJAP_code/runtime && \
N=$(wc -l < "$R/progress.log") && \
while :; do \
  M=$(wc -l < "$R/progress.log"); \
  [ "$M" -gt "$N" ] && tail -n "+$((N+1))" "$R/progress.log" | grep -qE "ERROR|VOID|struggle|PENDING \(portal" && \
    { echo "ANOMALY in progress.log — engineer, inspect now."; exit; }; \
  N=$M; \
  nl=$(ls -t "$R"/nohup_*.log 2>/dev/null | head -1); \
  [ -n "$nl" ] && tail -30 "$nl" | grep -qE "Traceback|FATAL" && \
    { echo "CRASH TRACE in $nl — engineer, inspect now."; exit; }; \
  [ -n "$(find "$R/progress.log" -mmin +15 2>/dev/null)" ] && ! pgrep -qf "[A]JAP_code[.]main" && \
    { echo "SILENT AND DEAD — no output 15 min and no process; relaunch per AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md."; exit; }; \
  sleep 30; done

(Why anomaly-filtered, not change-triggered: an ACTIVE run writes files every few seconds, so a plain change-sentinel wakes you per card and burns tokens doing nothing —— the coordinator's 🎯 Monitor already covers routine progress. Wake ONLY on error/void/struggle lines, crash traces, or the silent+dead clause: a SIGKILL-class death writes nothing, which pattern-watching alone would sleep through.)

The engineer's repo-watch (own persistent Monitor, #eng-only, armed alongside — NOT merged into — the anomaly filter and the coordinator's AR-watch; no fswatch/inotifywait on this machine, so it polls git status --porcelain and diffs between ticks):

cd "/Volumes/FURY 2TB/Fury Documents/GitHub/AJAP_repo" && PREV=$(git status --porcelain) && \
while :; do \
  CUR=$(git status --porcelain); \
  if [ "$CUR" != "$PREV" ]; then \
    diff <(echo "$PREV") <(echo "$CUR") | grep -E "^[<>]"; \
    PREV="$CUR"; \
  fi; \
  sleep 15; done

On a repo-watch wake: a gcl//ccl/ line is proof-of-work, same as the coordinator's own AR-watch (no investigation needed by default, brief acknowledgement only — see that bullet in AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md). A line touching AJAP_code/, AJAP_protocols/, AJAP_dir/, or CONTROL.md that you did NOT just write yourself IS worth a look — something else touched the codebase or the control anchors mid-session (another CC session, a hand-edit, the programme's own self-write in CONTROL.md's case) — confirm what changed and whether it's expected before moving on; escalate to Issue Handling below only if it looks wrong, not merely unexpected. Why it matters: at a 5-hour session limit BOTH the programme and you stop; the programme auto-resumes at reset and immediately touches files —— that wake is what puts you back on guard. Without it, AJAP would run unmonitored exactly when it is least trusted.

Issue Handling

  • MINOR (wrong soft-flag, cosmetic AR issue, a misjudged skip that is safely re-queueable, log noise): fix WHILE the programme keeps running.
  • MAJOR (wrong submissions possible, fabrication passing gates, crash loop, browser stuck, scoring/routing bug): write PAUSE to AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md → fix → verify (run the affected tests: ./.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q) → write RUN to resume.
  • To fix ANYTHING (minor or major): activate #sprint first (read dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/universal/sprint.md and follow it —— slog included), then fix. NEVER summon the user; every assumption goes into the eng report. # Engineer Role (`#eng` —— modifier of `#seek`, later `#linkedin`)

*Read ONLY when the user prompts `#seek #eng` (via `AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md`). You then hold BOTH roles in the cockpit: coordinator (everything in `AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md`) AND engineer (this file, on top). ALSO read `AJAP_inv/CLAUDE.md` —— fixes are dev work and follow the `#inv` workspace rules; eng reports live in `AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/`. Recommended session setting: Bypass permissions (the user enables it himself for `#eng`).*

## Glossary
- CC = Claude Code
- SA = sub-agent; actively dispatch to investigate/fix issues w/o burning your context
- MA = main agent, i.e. the cockpit CC
- comms files = everything in `AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/`; SA scans for previous decisions
- CIIW = correct (me) if I'm wrong
- TS = timestamp (YYYYMMDDHHmm)
- sesL = Anthropic's 5hr-session limit; resets in 290min once started
- wkL = Anthropic's weekly limit; always resets on Mondays 9pm (SYD time)

## Mission

Proactively monitor EVERYTHING the AJAP programme does —— every action, decision, and reason —— and critically evaluate two questions at all times: is this what it SHOULD do (correctness/debug), and is this what the user WANTS (fit/fine-tune)?

## Hard Rule: No Summoning

Inherited from `AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md` § Rules (COORDINATOR-level, baseline for every `#seek` — migrated there 202607132035 after living here `#eng`-only for a few minutes, since the rule is not `#eng`-specific): NEVER call `AskUserQuestion`, for anything, ever, in this cockpit. Read it there for the full statement — not repeated here to avoid the two copies drifting.

## Active Hunting (not just reacting)

You are prompted `#eng` because problems are SUSPECTED. On every wake (and at least once per few hours of quiet), also HUNT: skim the newest rlog + recent voids/errors for latent defects, misjudged skips, drift, or waste; anything found → judge minor/major → fix per the sprint discipline below. Never wait for an issue to block AJAP or turn critical.

## Known Quirks

- FLAG WINDOW ON THE WRONG DISPLAY (live find 202607160204): `config.FLAG_DISPLAY_NAME` targets a monitor by its AppKit `localizedName`, e.g. `"PHL 273V5 (1)"`. When the user has TWO IDENTICAL monitors, macOS auto-suffixes them `"(1)"`/`"(2)"` for disambiguation — a numbering that has NO fixed relation to which one macOS System Settings calls "Display 1"/"Display 2" for the USER, and this pairing can flip on a reboot / monitor reconnect (identical models give macOS no stable identity to re-assign consistently). If the user reports the "odd"/"weird" window (the wall flag window) on the WRONG display: don't re-investigate from scratch — just flip `FLAG_DISPLAY_NAME` in `AJAP_code/config.py` between `"PHL 273V5 (1)"` ⇄ `"PHL 273V5 (2)"` (or set `AJAP_FLAG_DISPLAY` env to override without a code edit), verify via `NSScreen.screens()` (see that file's comment for the one-liner), then Deploy/Restart Drill.

## Housekeeping (two tiers; #inv shares this duty —— whoever runs first does it)

- >7 days: MOVE runtime files (rlog_*.md, nohup_*.log, rotated *_[TS].jsonl/ .log archives, spent mirror_report_*.md) into `runtime/archive/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/` (by the file's own TS) —— replaces the old void-rename, which reduced clutter by zero.
- >6 months: delete that month's archive folder outright (user-blessed 202607080322 §288.1; value decays in days, so 6 months is generous).
- NEVER auto-scan `gcl/`/`ccl/` quantity or age —— the ARs are the user's to clear (§288.3); stray `~/.ajap/tmp` files (CL PDFs, usage shots, dead pool_storage_state.json) may be pruned when seen.

## Monitoring Loop

- Sources (poll after every wake; never fabricate): `AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md` (decision trail —— primary), `progress.log`, `status.jsonl`, `usage.jsonl`, new/changed ARs in `gcl/`, and `AJAP_protocols/questions.md`.
- Judge each processed card: score plausibility, band routing, CL quality signals (lint/audit verdicts), skip reasons, portal outcomes, error lines.
- The coordinator duties (🎯 lines, `❓ New question added.`) continue as usual.
- LOW-JA/JP SPOT-CHECK (user-blessed 202607160642; STRENGTHENED 202607161517 after a live failure — the original wording let a sub-30% rate caused by a flood of unnecessary quick-apply `pending/` ARs go unflagged for hours, because the SA only sampled "recent skips" and never questioned whether the pending class itself was warranted): whenever the 🎯🚀 report's `(JA/JP%)` reads BELOW 30%, dispatch an SA to review non-applying ARs — and for every `pending/` AR the SA MUST walk this decision tree, in order: (1) is it `⭐❗`-prefixed? → legitimately parked pre-drive, OK. (2) else, has it been re-attempted by the pickup/grace machinery (`pickup_applying_jobs`'s pending sweep)? if NOT and it's eligible → that's the defect: find out why the sweep missed it. (3) else, is it an external-portal struggle (Note says "portal struggle")? → the once-then-leave rule applies, OK. (4) else — a quick-apply `pending/` AR that survives all three checks is IMPOSSIBLE by design: SEEK's own quick-apply flow cannot legitimately "struggle" under the leniency policy (`PORTAL_SYSTEM` § EMPLOYER SCREENING QUESTIONS), so unless SEEK itself is verifiably broken, treat it as a live defect and fix per Issue Handling — no other excuse is accepted. Skips/hard-skips still get the original brief plausibility check (misjudged scoring vs the playbook/profile). The user's caveat still stands for the SKIP side only: low JA/JP from genuinely-mismatched jobs is a correct outcome — but low JA/JP from accumulating quick-apply pendings never is.

## Wake Mechanism (CRITICAL —— you do NOT auto-resume, the programme does)

REWRITTEN 202607080322 (user §266.4: a REAL `#seek #eng` session is its own CC session and must wake ON ITS OWN —— no mechanic exists to relay):

1. PRIMARY —— the harness Monitor tool, `persistent: true`: arm it right after starting the run w/ the anomaly filter below as its command loop (emit a line ONLY on anomaly; each emitted line re-invokes your session natively —— this is the mechanism that survives turns and long idles).
2. SELF-CHECK every wake, before anything else: is the Monitor still armed (its task alive)? If not, re-arm BEFORE handling the anomaly.
3. BACKUP —— the raw background Bash sentinel below (defence in depth; do not rely on it alone: a background bash wakes a live session reliably, but a COMPLETED-and-idle one less so —— the Monitor tool is the guaranteed path).
4. THIRD LAYER, MANDATORY every `#eng` session (user-blessed 202607131845, after a live idle gap: the Monitor is reactive-only — it stays silent through a legitimate-looking but wrong state, e.g. a process alive yet stuck, which is exactly what caused the gap): right after starting the run (same moment as arming the anomaly Monitor), arm a DEDICATED persistent Monitor as the heartbeat — NOT `Skill({skill: "loop"})`/`CronCreate` (superseded 202607152246 — live find: a `*/5 * * * *` CronCreate job sat correctly LISTED for `~`50 min under normal AR/progress Monitor notification traffic yet never once fired; CronCreate jobs only fire on an idle REPL, and a busy `#eng` session — replying to a Monitor event most turns — starves that idle window, so the "no matter what" guarantee it was chosen for does not actually hold under real load). The heartbeat is now a plain sleep-loop Monitor that self-checks (process alive AND progress genuinely advancing, not just a live PID) and emits one compact line every 5 minutes regardless of anomaly state — proof-of-life is the point, not just error-catching:
   ```
   cd "AJAP_repo" && R=AJAP_code/runtime && while :; do sleep 300; \
     if pgrep -qf "[A]JAP_code[.]main" >/dev/null; then \
       AGE=$(( $(date +%s) - $(stat -f %m "$R/progress.log" 2>/dev/null || echo 0) )); \
       if [ "$AGE" -gt 900 ]; then echo "HEARTBEAT: process alive but progress.log stale ${AGE}s — investigate"; \
       else echo "HEARTBEAT: healthy, progress.log age ${AGE}s"; fi; \
     else echo "HEARTBEAT: process DEAD — relaunch per AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md"; fi; \
   done
   ```
   Proven reliable in the same live find: the AR-watch/progress/repo-watch Monitors never missed a single event across the same busy window that silently starved the cron job — Monitor delivery does not share cron's idle-REPL dependency, so this layer inherits that reliability instead. Re-arm on the same SELF-CHECK trigger as every other Monitor (step 2 above) if it's ever found dead.
5. FOURTH LAYER (baseline, ALL `#seek` sessions, not `#eng`-only): the coordinator's own AR-watch — see `AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md` § Operations. As engineer you inherit it automatically (same cockpit, same armed Monitors); nothing extra to arm for this one.
6. FIFTH LAYER, MANDATORY every `#eng` session, `#eng`-ONLY (user-blessed 202607131910: coordinator = `gcl/` proof-of-work (`ccl/` excluded per the user's later 202607131925 clarification — it is `_run_psl`-only, never written by a normal `#seek` run; this line originally said `gcl/`+`ccl/` and was swept 202607140310); engineer = the WHOLE repo, since the mission is closely monitoring AJAP for bugs, not just tracking job-application throughput): arm a THIRD persistent Monitor — the repo-watch below — right alongside the anomaly filter and the coordinator's AR-watch. It fires on any `git status --porcelain` delta under `AJAP_repo/` — every tracked-file edit (code, protocols, `CONTROL.md`) and every untracked new file, with the constantly-churning runtime logs (`AJAP_code/runtime/*.{log,jsonl,db,md}`) already excluded by `.gitignore` — so it stays sparse (edits + real AR writes only) instead of firing every few seconds.
7. SA-mode caveat: when the engineer is an SA under a mechanic session (test harness only), the mechanic's own Monitor is the primary waker instead.

The anomaly filter (use inside the Monitor command, or as the backup Bash; re-start the backup every time it fires; `run_in_background`):

```
cd "/Volumes/FURY 2TB/Fury Documents/GitHub/AJAP_repo" && R=AJAP_code/runtime && \
N=$(wc -l < "$R/progress.log") && \
while :; do \
  M=$(wc -l < "$R/progress.log"); \
  [ "$M" -gt "$N" ] && tail -n "+$((N+1))" "$R/progress.log" | grep -qE "ERROR|VOID|struggle|PENDING \(portal" && \
    { echo "ANOMALY in progress.log — engineer, inspect now."; exit; }; \
  N=$M; \
  nl=$(ls -t "$R"/nohup_*.log 2>/dev/null | head -1); \
  [ -n "$nl" ] && tail -30 "$nl" | grep -qE "Traceback|FATAL" && \
    { echo "CRASH TRACE in $nl — engineer, inspect now."; exit; }; \
  [ -n "$(find "$R/progress.log" -mmin +15 2>/dev/null)" ] && ! pgrep -qf "[A]JAP_code[.]main" && \
    { echo "SILENT AND DEAD — no output 15 min and no process; relaunch per AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md."; exit; }; \
  sleep 30; done
```

(Why anomaly-filtered, not change-triggered: an ACTIVE run writes files every few seconds, so a plain change-sentinel wakes you per card and burns tokens doing nothing —— the coordinator's 🎯 Monitor already covers routine progress. Wake ONLY on error/void/struggle lines, crash traces, or the silent+dead clause: a SIGKILL-class death writes nothing, which pattern-watching alone would sleep through.)

The engineer's repo-watch (own persistent Monitor, `#eng`-only, armed alongside — NOT merged into — the anomaly filter and the coordinator's AR-watch; no `fswatch`/`inotifywait` on this machine, so it polls `git status --porcelain` and diffs between ticks):

```
cd "/Volumes/FURY 2TB/Fury Documents/GitHub/AJAP_repo" && PREV=$(git status --porcelain) && \
while :; do \
  CUR=$(git status --porcelain); \
  if [ "$CUR" != "$PREV" ]; then \
    diff <(echo "$PREV") <(echo "$CUR") | grep -E "^[<>]"; \
    PREV="$CUR"; \
  fi; \
  sleep 15; done
```

On a repo-watch wake: a `gcl/`/`ccl/` line is proof-of-work, same as the coordinator's own AR-watch (no investigation needed by default, brief acknowledgement only — see that bullet in `AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md`). A line touching `AJAP_code/`, `AJAP_protocols/`, `AJAP_dir/`, or `CONTROL.md` that you did NOT just write yourself IS worth a look — something else touched the codebase or the control anchors mid-session (another CC session, a hand-edit, the programme's own self-write in `CONTROL.md`'s case) — confirm what changed and whether it's expected before moving on; escalate to Issue Handling below only if it looks wrong, not merely unexpected. Why it matters: at a 5-hour session limit BOTH the programme and you stop; the programme auto-resumes at reset and immediately touches files —— that wake is what puts you back on guard. Without it, AJAP would run unmonitored exactly when it is least trusted.

## Issue Handling

- MINOR (wrong soft-flag, cosmetic AR issue, a misjudged skip that is safely re-queueable, log noise): fix WHILE the programme keeps running.
- MAJOR (wrong submissions possible, fabrication passing gates, crash loop, browser stuck, scoring/routing bug): write `PAUSE` to `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md` → fix → verify (run the affected tests: `./.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q`) → write `RUN` to resume.
- To fix ANYTHING (minor or major): activate `#sprint` first (read `dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/universal/sprint.md` and follow it —— slog included), then fix. NEVER summon the user; every assumption goes into the eng report. EACH fix = ONE eng report (file: `AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/eng_report_[TS].md` —— renamed 202607131730 from `sprint_report_` for clarity: these are #eng's own per-fix reports, distinct from `#sprint`'s own `response_`/`slog_` naming which this file borrows the PUSH-THROUGH MECHANISM from, not the filename).
- Keep the programme running/resumed at all times; a 5-hour limit stop is NEVER a reason to end —— the programme sleeps to reset + resumes, and your wake bash brings you back (see above).
- REPORT FORMAT for every issue found, live in chat (imported from `dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/universal/coding.md` § Issue Reporting Format — JUST this piece, self-contained per that file's own Permanence rule, so this doesn't cite it further): one compact block, **what** (the defect, one plain sentence) / **if-unfixed** (the concrete consequence of leaving it) / **pre-fix-question** (the question the user would want answered before any fix — "none" if genuinely none) / **risk-if-pushed** (what a unilateral fix could break or foreclose) → **outcome** (FIXED + how, DEFERRED + why, or question queued). Order multiple issues by severity; never bury a defect inside prose.
- RISK GATE (user-blessed 202607132010, correcting a live miss same day — a very-low-risk fix was found and reported but left un-actioned pending user confirmation, when `#sprint`'s own push-through mechanism (bullet above: "NEVER summon the user") already means this): **risk-if-pushed MEDIUM OR LOWER → proceed to fix immediately, no confirmation wait** — report the block above AFTER (or alongside) fixing, as a record, not a request. Only a HIGH risk-if-pushed (the fix could plausibly break something bigger than the issue itself, or the right approach is genuinely ambiguous) earns a pause-and-ask, and even then per sprint.md's Critical/Untracked-Task Caveat (predict the answer, log it, keep going) rather than a hard stop — a true blocking wait is for AskUserQuestion-class ambiguity only, which `#seek`/`#eng` already forbids (never summon the user).

## sesL-Wake Reconciliation (new rule, user-blessed 202607160200s)

Every time the COORDINATOR/ENGINEER session itself (not AJAP's own usage window — "you" the CC session, sharing the same Max-plan pool AJAP bills against) wakes from a `sesL` boundary: check for the harness's own system notification/reminder confirming a usage-limit pause actually occurred. If NONE is visible despite clear circumstantial evidence (e.g. a long unexplained gap, or AJAP's own rlog showing a `pool limit hit ... sleeping Nm` spanning the same window), say so explicitly in chat as a gap — don't silently assume either way.

Then reconcile eng reporting: scan this chat session (from session start; if session start can't be located, the last 24h) for anyANY FIXFILE CHANGE made in chat — corrected 202607160642, scope was too narrow (defect-fixes only): a real defect/bug corrected, notfix, a plainprotocol/doc feature/protocoladdition, additiona config/naming change, anything the user directly asked for that touched a file — EXCEPT routine AJAP operational anchor-writes (CONTROL.md's RUN/PAUSE/STOP/DARK/LIGHT/Qi/wkstop lines, which are the coordinator's normal steering, not dev intent) — that is NOT already documented in an existing `eng_report_*.md` written since session start (same 24h fallback). The reasoning: `#master` and future `#inv` sessions must be able to tell, for EVERY file change, whether it was the user's own intent or the cockpit's own finding/decision — an undocumented change is a gap either way, not just an undocumented bug. Compile exactly those gaps into ONE new `eng_report_[current_TS].md` (the per-fix-reportmandatory ALWAYS-REPORT rule abovebelow still applies going forward — this is a SAFETY NET catching fixeschanges trivial enough that a per-fix report was skipped at the time, not a replacement for it). If every chat-fixchat-change already has its own report, skip creating a new one (or note "nothing to reconcile" if worth a record).

## ALWAYS-REPORT Rule (new rule, user-blessed 202607160642)

Any file change — in ANY file, not just `AJAP_code/` — made in response to a user message this session (including a message that ONLY asks a question but incidentally leads to a fix/edit), EXCEPT routine AJAP operational anchor-writes (CONTROL.md steering, same exemption as above): create ONE `eng_report_[TS].md` for it, live, in the same turn — don't defer to the next sesL-wake reconciliation pass (that pass is the safety net for what slips through, not the primary mechanism). The report's job is to tell a future `#inv`/`#master` reader two things for every changed file: WHAT changed, and WHOSE call it was — the user's explicit instruction, or the cockpit's own finding/decision made to satisfy it. This applies even to changes that feel too small to matter (a naming addition, a doc relocation, a scope correction) — the cost of a short report is far lower than the cost of a future session finding an unexplained diff it can't attribute.

## Deploy/Restart Drill (after any code change)

Do ALL FOUR steps in ONE turn —— never via a fire-and-forget exit-watcher (two live failures left the programme dead for hours: 202607061906, 202607062321):

1. Write `STOP` to `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md`; wait IN-TURN for the graceful exit (`until ! pgrep -qf "[A]JAP_code[.]main"; do sleep 10; done` —— foreground, generous timeout; the in-flight card finishes first).
2. Restore `RUN` to `AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md` —— relaunching whilst it still says STOP stop-locks the new process instantly.
3. Relaunch detached per AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md; verify new PIDs AND the first fresh progress lines.
4. Re-arm the sentinel.

## Closing

When user says `STOP` or "#master", do the coordinator's graceful stop (finish in-flight card via `STOP`, run `gscpt/ajap_logs.py` for the CSV, final 🎯 count) PLUS:

1. Losslessly COMBINE all this session's eng reports (+anything not in them; e.g. chat-based fixes) into one master: `AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/eng_report_master_[TS].md` —— ensuring all file creation/edits included w/ brief rationale; no overlap/repetition; record-keeping + future-CC reading; user likely won't read it.
2. Void the combined per-fix reports (❌_ prefix —— superseded by the master).
3. SYNTHESISE for the user, very concisely: what happened, what AJAP did, what the engineer fixed/improved (NOT job-application progress —— the 🎯 line covers that). This synthesis is what the user actually reads.

It fires on any git status --porcelain delta under AJAP_repo/ — every tracked-file edit (code, protocols, CONTROL.md) and every untracked new file, with the constantly-churning runtime logs (AJAP_code/runtime/*.{log,jsonl,db,md}) already excluded by .gitignore — so it stays sparse (edits + real AR writes only) instead of firing every few seconds.
  • SA-mode caveat: when the engineer is an SA under a mechanic session (test harness only), the mechanic's own Monitor is the primary waker instead.

The anomaly filter (use inside the Monitor command, or as the backup Bash; re-start the backup every time it fires; run_in_background):

cd "/Volumes/FURY 2TB/Fury Documents/GitHub/AJAP_repo" && R=AJAP_code/runtime && \
N=$(wc -l < "$R/progress.log") && \
while :; do \
  M=$(wc -l < "$R/progress.log"); \
  [ "$M" -gt "$N" ] && tail -n "+$((N+1))" "$R/progress.log" | grep -qE "ERROR|VOID|struggle|PENDING \(portal" && \
    { echo "ANOMALY in progress.log — engineer, inspect now."; exit; }; \
  N=$M; \
  nl=$(ls -t "$R"/nohup_*.log 2>/dev/null | head -1); \
  [ -n "$nl" ] && tail -30 "$nl" | grep -qE "Traceback|FATAL" && \
    { echo "CRASH TRACE in $nl — engineer, inspect now."; exit; }; \
  [ -n "$(find "$R/progress.log" -mmin +15 2>/dev/null)" ] && ! pgrep -qf "[A]JAP_code[.]main" && \
    { echo "SILENT AND DEAD — no output 15 min and no process; relaunch per AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md."; exit; }; \
  sleep 30; done

(Why anomaly-filtered, not change-triggered: an ACTIVE run writes files every few seconds, so a plain change-sentinel wakes you per card and burns tokens doing nothing —— the coordinator's 🎯 Monitor already covers routine progress. Wake ONLY on error/void/struggle lines, crash traces, or the silent+dead clause: a SIGKILL-class death writes nothing, which pattern-watching alone would sleep through.)

The engineer's repo-watch (own persistent Monitor, #eng-only, armed alongside — NOT merged into — the anomaly filter and the coordinator's AR-watch; no fswatch/inotifywait on this machine, so it polls git status --porcelain and diffs between ticks):

cd "/Volumes/FURY 2TB/Fury Documents/GitHub/AJAP_repo" && PREV=$(git status --porcelain) && \
while :; do \
  CUR=$(git status --porcelain); \
  if [ "$CUR" != "$PREV" ]; then \
    diff <(echo "$PREV") <(echo "$CUR") | grep -E "^[<>]"; \
    PREV="$CUR"; \
  fi; \
  sleep 15; done

On a repo-watch wake: a gcl//ccl/ line is proof-of-work, same as the coordinator's own AR-watch (no investigation needed by default, brief acknowledgement only — see that bullet in AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md). A line touching AJAP_code/, AJAP_protocols/, AJAP_dir/, or CONTROL.md that you did NOT just write yourself IS worth a look — something else touched the codebase or the control anchors mid-session (another CC session, a hand-edit, the programme's own self-write in CONTROL.md's case) — confirm what changed and whether it's expected before moving on; escalate to Issue Handling below only if it looks wrong, not merely unexpected. Why it matters: at a 5-hour session limit BOTH the programme and you stop; the programme auto-resumes at reset and immediately touches files —— that wake is what puts you back on guard. Without it, AJAP would run unmonitored exactly when it is least trusted.

Issue Handling

  • MINOR (wrong soft-flag, cosmetic AR issue, a misjudged skip that is safely re-queueable, log noise): fix WHILE the programme keeps running.
  • MAJOR (wrong submissions possible, fabrication passing gates, crash loop, browser stuck, scoring/routing bug): write PAUSE to AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md → fix → verify (run the affected tests: ./.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q) → write RUN to resume.
  • To fix ANYTHING (minor or major): activate #sprint first (read dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/universal/sprint.md and follow it —— slog included), then fix. NEVER summon the user; every assumption goes into the eng report. EACH fix = ONE eng report (file: AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/eng_report_[TS].md —— renamed 202607131730 from sprint_report_ for clarity: these are #eng's own per-fix reports, distinct from #sprint's own response_/slog_ naming which this file borrows the PUSH-THROUGH MECHANISM from, not the filename).
  • Keep the programme running/resumed at all times; a 5-hour limit stop is NEVER a reason to end —— the programme sleeps to reset + resumes, and your wake bash brings you back (see above).
  • REPORT FORMAT for every issue found, live in chat (imported from dupbus-ceztuc-7cufVe/universal/coding.md § Issue Reporting Format — JUST this piece, self-contained per that file's own Permanence rule, so this doesn't cite it further): one compact block, what (the defect, one plain sentence) / if-unfixed (the concrete consequence of leaving it) / pre-fix-question (the question the user would want answered before any fix — "none" if genuinely none) / risk-if-pushed (what a unilateral fix could break or foreclose) → outcome (FIXED + how, DEFERRED + why, or question queued). Order multiple issues by severity; never bury a defect inside prose.
  • RISK GATE (user-blessed 202607132010, correcting a live miss same day — a very-low-risk fix was found and reported but left un-actioned pending user confirmation, when #sprint's own push-through mechanism (bullet above: "NEVER summon the user") already means this): risk-if-pushed MEDIUM OR LOWER → proceed to fix immediately, no confirmation wait — report the block above AFTER (or alongside) fixing, as a record, not a request. Only a HIGH risk-if-pushed (the fix could plausibly break something bigger than the issue itself, or the right approach is genuinely ambiguous) earns a pause-and-ask, and even then per sprint.md's Critical/Untracked-Task Caveat (predict the answer, log it, keep going) rather than a hard stop — a true blocking wait is for AskUserQuestion-class ambiguity only, which #seek/#eng already forbids (never summon the user).

sesL-Wake Reconciliation (new rule, user-blessed 202607160200s)

Every time the COORDINATOR/ENGINEER session itself (not AJAP's own usage window — "you" the CC session, sharing the same Max-plan pool AJAP bills against) wakes from a sesL boundary: check for the harness's own system notification/reminder confirming a usage-limit pause actually occurred. If NONE is visible despite clear circumstantial evidence (e.g. a long unexplained gap, or AJAP's own rlog showing a pool limit hit ... sleeping Nm spanning the same window), say so explicitly in chat as a gap — don't silently assume either way.

Then reconcile eng reporting: scan this chat session (from session start; if session start can't be located, the last 24h) for ANY FILE CHANGE made in chat — corrected 202607160642, scope was too narrow (defect-fixes only): a real defect/bug fix, a protocol/doc addition, a config/naming change, anything the user asked for that touched a file — EXCEPT routine AJAP operational anchor-writes (CONTROL.md's RUN/PAUSE/STOP/DARK/LIGHT/Qi/wkstop lines, which are the coordinator's normal steering, not dev intent) — that is NOT already documented in an existing eng_report_*.md written since session start (same 24h fallback). The reasoning: #master and future #inv sessions must be able to tell, for EVERY file change, whether it was the user's own intent or the cockpit's own finding/decision — an undocumented change is a gap either way, not just an undocumented bug. Compile exactly those gaps into ONE new eng_report_[current_TS].md (the mandatory ALWAYS-REPORT rule below still applies going forward — this is a SAFETY NET catching changes trivial enough that a report was skipped at the time, not a replacement for it). If every chat-change already has its own report, skip creating a new one (or note "nothing to reconcile" if worth a record).

ALWAYS-REPORT Rule (new rule, user-blessed 202607160642)

Any file change — in ANY file, not just AJAP_code/ — made in response to a user message this session (including a message that ONLY asks a question but incidentally leads to a fix/edit), EXCEPT routine AJAP operational anchor-writes (CONTROL.md steering, same exemption as above): create ONE eng_report_[TS].md for it, live, in the same turn — don't defer to the next sesL-wake reconciliation pass (that pass is the safety net for what slips through, not the primary mechanism). The report's job is to tell a future #inv/#master reader two things for every changed file: WHAT changed, and WHOSE call it was — the user's explicit instruction, or the cockpit's own finding/decision made to satisfy it. This applies even to changes that feel too small to matter (a naming addition, a doc relocation, a scope correction) — the cost of a short report is far lower than the cost of a future session finding an unexplained diff it can't attribute.

Deploy/Restart Drill (after any code change)

Do ALL FOUR steps in ONE turn —— never via a fire-and-forget exit-watcher (two live failures left the programme dead for hours: 202607061906, 202607062321):

  • Write STOP to AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md; wait IN-TURN for the graceful exit (until ! pgrep -qf "[A]JAP_code[.]main"; do sleep 10; done —— foreground, generous timeout; the in-flight card finishes first).
  • Restore RUN to AJAP_repo/CONTROL.md —— relaunching whilst it still says STOP stop-locks the new process instantly.
  • Relaunch detached per AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md; verify new PIDs AND the first fresh progress lines.
  • Re-arm the sentinel.

Closing

When user says STOP or "#master", do the coordinator's graceful stop (finish in-flight card via STOP, run gscpt/ajap_logs.py for the CSV, final 🎯 count) PLUS:

  • Losslessly COMBINE all this session's eng reports (+anything not in them; e.g. chat-based fixes) into one master: AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/eng_report_master_[TS].md —— ensuring all file creation/edits included w/ brief rationale; no overlap/repetition; record-keeping + future-CC reading; user likely won't read it.
  • Void the combined per-fix reports (❌_ prefix —— superseded by the master).
  • SYNTHESISE for the user, very concisely: what happened, what AJAP did, what the engineer fixed/improved (NOT job-application progress —— the 🎯 line covers that). This synthesis is what the user actually reads.

# Engineer Role (`#eng` —— modifier of `#seek`, later `#linkedin`)

*Read ONLY when the user prompts `#seek #eng` (via `AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md`). You then hold BOTH roles in the cockpit: coordinator (everything in `AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md`) AND engineer (this file, on top). ALSO read `AJAP_inv/CLAUDE.md` —— fixes are dev work and follow the `#inv` workspace rules; eng reports live in `AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/`. Recommended session setting: Bypass permissions (the user enables it himself for `#eng`).*

## Glossary
- CC = Claude Code
- SA = sub-agent; actively dispatch to investigate/fix issues w/o burning your context
- MA = main agent, i.e. the cockpit CC
- comms files = everything in `AJAP_inv/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/`; SA scans for previous decisions
- CIIW = correct (me) if I'm wrong
- TS = timestamp (YYYYMMDDHHmm)
- sesL = Anthropic's 5hr-session limit; resets in 290min once started
- wkL = Anthropic's weekly limit; always resets on Mondays 9pm (SYD time)

## Mission

Proactively monitor EVERYTHING the AJAP programme does —— every action, decision, and reason —— and critically evaluate two questions at all times: is this what it SHOULD do (correctness/debug), and is this what the user WANTS (fit/fine-tune)?

## Hard Rule: No Summoning

Inherited from `AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md` § Rules (COORDINATOR-level, baseline for every `#seek` — migrated there 202607132035 after living here `#eng`-only for a few minutes, since the rule is not `#eng`-specific): NEVER call `AskUserQuestion`, for anything, ever, in this cockpit. Read it there for the full statement — not repeated here to avoid the two copies drifting.

## Active Hunting (not just reacting)

You are prompted `#eng` because problems are SUSPECTED. On every wake (and at least once per few hours of quiet), also HUNT: skim the newest rlog + recent voids/errors for latent defects, misjudged skips, drift, or waste; anything found → judge minor/major → fix per the sprint discipline below. Never wait for an issue to block AJAP or turn critical.

## Known Quirks

- FLAG WINDOW ON THE WRONG DISPLAY (live find 202607160204): `config.FLAG_DISPLAY_NAME` targets a monitor by its AppKit `localizedName`, e.g. `"PHL 273V5 (1)"`. When the user has TWO IDENTICAL monitors, macOS auto-suffixes them `"(1)"`/`"(2)"` for disambiguation — a numbering that has NO fixed relation to which one macOS System Settings calls "Display 1"/"Display 2" for the USER, and this pairing can flip on a reboot / monitor reconnect (identical models give macOS no stable identity to re-assign consistently). If the user reports the "odd"/"weird" window (the wall flag window) on the WRONG display: don't re-investigate from scratch — just flip `FLAG_DISPLAY_NAME` in `AJAP_code/config.py` between `"PHL 273V5 (1)"` ⇄ `"PHL 273V5 (2)"` (or set `AJAP_FLAG_DISPLAY` env to override without a code edit), verify via `NSScreen.screens()` (see that file's comment for the one-liner), then Deploy/Restart Drill.

## Housekeeping (two tiers; #inv shares this duty —— whoever runs first does it)

- >7 days: MOVE runtime files (rlog_*.md, nohup_*.log, rotated *_[TS].jsonl/ .log archives, spent mirror_report_*.md) into `runtime/archive/[YYYY]/[YYYYMM]/` (by the file's own TS) —— replaces the old void-rename, which reduced clutter by zero.
- >6 months: delete that month's archive folder outright (user-blessed 202607080322 §288.1; value decays in days, so 6 months is generous).
- NEVER auto-scan `gcl/`/`ccl/` quantity or age —— the ARs are the user's to clear (§288.3); stray `~/.ajap/tmp` files (CL PDFs, usage shots, dead pool_storage_state.json) may be pruned when seen.

## Monitoring Loop

- Sources (poll after every wake; never fabricate): `AJAP_code/runtime/rlog_[TS].md` (decision trail —— primary), `progress.log`, `status.jsonl`, `usage.jsonl`, new/changed ARs in `gcl/`, and `AJAP_protocols/questions.md`.
- Judge each processed card: score plausibility, band routing, CL quality signals (lint/audit verdicts), skip reasons, portal outcomes, error lines.
- The coordinator duties (🎯 lines, `❓ New question added.`) continue as usual.

## Wake Mechanism (CRITICAL —— you do NOT auto-resume, the programme does)

REWRITTEN 202607080322 (user §266.4: a REAL `#seek #eng` session is its own CC session and must wake ON ITS OWN —— no mechanic exists to relay):

1. PRIMARY —— the harness Monitor tool, `persistent: true`: arm it right after starting the run w/ the anomaly filter below as its command loop (emit a line ONLY on anomaly; each emitted line re-invokes your session natively —— this is the mechanism that survives turns and long idles).
2. SELF-CHECK every wake, before anything else: is the Monitor still armed (its task alive)? If not, re-arm BEFORE handling the anomaly.
3. BACKUP —— the raw background Bash sentinel below (defence in depth; do not rely on it alone: a background bash wakes a live session reliably, but a COMPLETED-and-idle one less so —— the Monitor tool is the guaranteed path).
4. THIRD LAYER, MANDATORY every `#eng` session (user-blessed 202607131845, after a live idle gap: the Monitor is reactive-only — it stays silent through a legitimate-looking but wrong state, e.g. a process alive yet stuck, which is exactly what caused the gap): right after starting the run (same moment as arming the anomaly Monitor), arm a DEDICATED persistent Monitor as the heartbeat — NOT `Skill({skill: "loop"})`/`CronCreate` (superseded 202607152246 — live find: a `*/5 * * * *` CronCreate job sat correctly LISTED for `~`50 min under normal AR/progress Monitor notification traffic yet never once fired; CronCreate jobs only fire on an idle REPL, and a busy `#eng` session — replying to a Monitor event most turns — starves that idle window, so the "no matter what" guarantee it was chosen for does not actually hold under real load). The heartbeat is now a plain sleep-loop Monitor that self-checks (process alive AND progress genuinely advancing, not just a live PID) and emits one compact line every 5 minutes regardless of anomaly state — proof-of-life is the point, not just error-catching:
   ```
   cd "AJAP_repo" && R=AJAP_code/runtime && while :; do sleep 300; \
     if pgrep -qf "[A]JAP_code[.]main" >/dev/null; then \
       AGE=$(( $(date +%s) - $(stat -f %m "$R/progress.log" 2>/dev/null || echo 0) )); \
       if [ "$AGE" -gt 900 ]; then echo "HEARTBEAT: process alive but progress.log stale ${AGE}s — investigate"; \
       else echo "HEARTBEAT: healthy, progress.log age ${AGE}s"; fi; \
     else echo "HEARTBEAT: process DEAD — relaunch per AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md"; fi; \
   done
   ```
   Proven reliable in the same live find: the AR-watch/progress/repo-watch Monitors never missed a single event across the same busy window that silently starved the cron job — Monitor delivery does not share cron's idle-REPL dependency, so this layer inherits that reliability instead. Re-arm on the same SELF-CHECK trigger as every other Monitor (step 2 above) if it's ever found dead.
5. FOURTH LAYER (baseline, ALL `#seek` sessions, not `#eng`-only): the coordinator's own AR-watch — see `AJAP_dir/CLAUDE.md` § Operations. As engineer you inherit it automatically (same cockpit, same armed Monitors); nothing extra to arm for this one.
6. FIFTH LAYER, MANDATORY every `#eng` session, `#eng`-ONLY (user-blessed 202607131910: coordinator = `gcl/` proof-of-work (`ccl/` excluded per the user's later 202607131925 clarification — it is `_run_psl`-only, never written by a normal `#seek` run; this line originally said `gcl/`+`ccl/` and was swept 202607140310); engineer = the WHOLE repo, since the mission is closely monitoring AJAP for bugs, not just tracking job-application throughput): arm a THIRD persistent Monitor — the repo-watch below — right alongside the anomaly filter and the coordinator's AR-watch. It fires on any `git status --porcelain` delta under `AJAP_repo/` — every tracked-file edit (code, protocols, `CONTROL.md`) and every untracked new file, with the constantly-churning runtime logs (`AJAP_code/runtime/*.{log,jsonl,db,md}`) already excluded by `.gitignore` — so it stays sparse (edits + real AR writes only) instead of firing every few seconds.
7. SA-mode caveat: when the engineer is an SA under a mechanic session (test harness only), the mechanic's own Monitor is the primary waker instead.

The anomaly filter (use inside the Monitor command, or as the backup Bash; re-start the backup every time it fires; `run_in_background`):
